On L-functions of quadratic Q-curves

Coverage: Entire pinned arXiv1511.09001v2, all41pages and all59references independently inspected after handoff. Plans003–031 record the full actual reading inventory, statement cones, accessible immediate theorem proof lists and precise unresolved source claims.

REQUIRED CONCEPT FRONTIER (42)

- Analytic rank of an elliptic curve: For an elliptic curve E over a number field K whose Hasse–Weil L-function is holomorphic near s=1, its analytic rank is ord_(s=1)L(E/K,s). The definition is conditional on the needed continuation; it does not assert general modularity or equality with the Mordell–Weil rank.
- BSD period of an elliptic curve over a number field: For E/K choose a nonzero invariant differential ω and additive Haar measures dx_v whose product on𝔄_K gives vol(𝔄_K/K)=1. Its BSD period is P(E/K)=∏_(v finite)[L_v(E,1)∫_(E(K_v))|ω|_v]·∏_(v infinite)∫_(E(K_v))|ω|_v. Almost all finite factors are1; the product formula makes the result independent of ω, and the adelic normalization fixes its discriminant factor.
- Conductor formula for Weil restriction of an abelian variety: For an abelian variety A/L of dimension g and a finite extension of number fields L/K, 𝔑_K(Res_(L/K)A)=Norm_(L/K)(𝔑_L(A))·𝔡_(L/K)^(2g). In particular for an elliptic curve over quadratic K/ℚ the rational conductor is the absolute norm of its K-conductor times Δ_K².
- Conductor of the abelian variety attached to a newform: For a normalized weight-two newform f of level N and coefficient field F, its associated abelian variety A_f/ℚ has conductor N^[F:ℚ]. The integer N is the primitive newform level, not a chosen oldform ambient level.
- Cyclotomic Galois action on µ_N-level cusps: On the µ_N arithmetic model of X₁(N), and its quotients associated to Γ₁(N)⊆Γ⊆Γ₀(N), every cusp is defined over ℚ(ζ_N). If σ_d(ζ_N)=ζ_N^d, it sends the cusp with primitive residue pair(x,y) modulo N to the cusp represented by any primitive integer lift of(x,d⁻¹y) modulo N. The result is read as a cusp residue class, not by cancelling a nonprimitive fraction; in particular∞ is rational.
- Denominator ideal of an element of a number field: Forα in a number field K, the denominator ideal isD_α={a∈O_K:aα∈O_K}. It is a nonzero integral ideal ofO_K and is the largest integral ideal whose product withα lies inO_K. This definition uses the full ring of integers rather than a chosen possibly nonmaximal order.
- Determinant of the Tate representation of a weight-two newform: For a normalized weight-two newform f of level N and nebentypusχ, V_(f,λ) has dimension two over F_λ and detρ_(f,λ)=χ·χ_ℓ as characters of G_Q, whereχ is viewed as a Galois character andχ_ℓ is the ℓ-adic cyclotomic character. This character identity applies on every decomposition group, including primes dividing N.
- Effective prime-isogeny enumeration over a number field: For a geometrically non-CM elliptic curve E/K over a number field, an algorithm determines every rational primep admitting aK-defined cyclicp-isogeny fromE and computes all such isogenies up to targetK-isomorphism.
- Elliptic building blocks from inner-twist splitting maps: Let f be a non-CM weight-two newform with abelian coefficient field F, whose inner twists range over Gal(F/ℚ), and suppose its Gauss-sum cocycle c(σ,τ)=g(χ_σ⁻¹)g(σχ_τ⁻¹)/g(χ_(στ)⁻¹) is the coboundary of β:Gal(F/ℚ)→F×. Over the inner-twist splitting field L, there is w_β∈End_L(A_f) whose image is an elliptic building block and whose differential line pulls back to the span of ∑_σ[g(χ_σ⁻¹)/β(σ)]σf. All elliptic building blocks arise by applying coefficient-field endomorphisms to this image.
- Gauss sum of a primitive quadratic character: For a fundamental discriminant D and χ_D, ∑_(a mod|D|)χ_D(a)exp(2πina/|D|)=χ_D(n)√D for every positive n, where√D is the positive real square root when D>0 and i√|D| when D<0. In particular the Gauss sum at n=1 has this exact value.
- Generalized Manin conjecture for Q-curves: For an optimal modular parametrization π:X₁(N)→E of a ℚ-curve completely defined over its base number field, the Manin ideal c(π) is the unit ideal.
- Greenberg filtration of a smooth group over a truncated DVR: Let 𝒢 be a smooth commutative group scheme over a DVR S with residue field k and maximal ideal 𝔪, and suppose the k-algebra S/𝔪^e is identified with k[t]/(t^e). Assume the indicated Weil restrictions are representable. In X=Res_((S/𝔪^e)/k)(𝒢_(S/𝔪^e)), let F⁰X=X and F^iX be the kernel of reduction to 𝒢 modulo t^i for1≤i≤e. Then Gr⁰X≅𝒢_k and Gr^iX≅Lie(𝒢_k)⊗_k(𝔪/𝔪²)^(⊗i) for1≤i<e, as additive vector groups.
- Hilbert’s theorem90: For a field F with separable closure Fsep, H¹(Gal(Fsep/F),(Fsep)×)=0. Equivalently, for a finite Galois extension L/F every multiplicative1-cocycle c_σ is b⁻¹σ(b) for some b∈L×.
- Inner twist and nebentypus of a quadratic Q-curve newform: Let E/K be a geometrically non-CM completely defined quadraticQ-curve with conjugate isogenyµ andνµµ=[m], where m is nonsquare, and let f be the associated newform with coefficient fieldF=Q(√m). Its nontrivial coefficient conjugationσ satisfiesσ(a_p)=χ_K(p)a_p atp outside the level, whereχ_K is the primitive quadratic character ofK. The nebentypus of f is trivial when m>0 and isχ_K, viewed modulo the level, when m<0.
- Integral basis of a quadratic number field: Letd≠1 be a nonzero squarefree integer andF=Q(√d). ThenO_F=Z[√d] ifd≡2 or3 mod4, andO_F=Z[(1+√d)/2] ifd≡1 mod4. The squarefree assumption on the chosen radicand is essential; the same formulas with an arbitrary merely nonsquarem are not valid.
- Integrality of the Manin ideal: For a modular parametrization π:X₁^µ(N)→E of a completely defined non-CM ℚ-curve over a number field K, with q-expansion at the rational cusp of the µ_N arithmetic model, its Manin ideal c(π) is an integral O_K-ideal.
- Integrality of the X₀ Manin constant: For a modular parametrizationπ:X₀(N)→E/Q at the conductor level and a global minimal differentialω_E, the signed Manin constant c(π) is a nonzero integer. In particular the unsigned constant of the optimal quotient is a positive integer.
- Integration pairing for weight-two modular symbols: For f∈S₂(Γ₁(N)), the assignment{a,b}↦∫_a^b f(z)dz defines a well-defined complex-linear integration pairing withM₂(Γ₁(N))⊗C. The integrals at cusps converge, depend only on the modular-symbol class, and are additive in both the cusp form and the symbol.
- Isogeny graph of a non-CM elliptic curve over a number field: For a geometrically non-CM elliptic curve E/K, its K-isogeny graph has vertices the K-isomorphism classes K-isogenous toE and an edge labeledp between two vertices when there is aK-defined cyclic isogeny of prime degreep between them. Dual isogenies make the edges undirected; the graph is finite and connected, and traversing prime-degree isogenies enumerates its vertices.
- Kato rank-zero criterion for a modular abelian quotient: Let A/Q be an abelian variety admitting a surjective homomorphism J₁(N)→A for some N. If L(A/Q,1)≠0, then A(Q) is finite. The assertion is a theorem and does not assume the full Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture.
- Manin conjecture for an optimal X₀ elliptic quotient: For an optimal modular parametrizationX₀(N)→E/Q at the conductor level, the unsigned Manin constant is1, or equivalently the signed constant attached to a chosen minimal differential is±1. This is a conjecture, not asserted by its definition or by integrality.
- Manin–Drinfel’d theorem on cuspidal divisor classes: For a compact modular curve associated to a congruence subgroup, every degree-zero divisor supported on its cusps has torsion class in its Jacobian. In particular a modular parametrization sends the difference of two cusps to a torsion point on an elliptic curve.
- Mellin formula for a weight-two newform: For a normalized weight-two newform f of level N, Λ(f,s)=N^(s/2)(2π)^(-s)Γ(s)L(f,s)=N^(s/2)∫₀^∞f(it)t^(s−1)dt. The integral converges locally uniformly for all complex s and gives an entire continuation; at s=1 it gives L(f,1)=−2πi∫₀^(i∞)f(z)dz.
- Mordell–Weil rank of an elliptic curve: For an elliptic curve E over a number field F, its Mordell–Weil rank is dim_ℚ(ℚ⊗_ℤE(F)), equivalently the number of infinite cyclic factors in E(F) when finite generation is known. It concerns rational points and is distinct from analytic rank defined by an L-function zero.
- Mordell–Weil theorem for elliptic curves: For an elliptic curve E over a number field F, E(F) is a finitely generated abelian group, hence is isomorphic to E(F)_tors⊕ℤʳ for a finite nonnegative integerr.
- Néron identity fiber in multiplicative elliptic reduction: For an elliptic curve E over a nonarchimedean local field K with residue field k, multiplicative reduction means that the identity component of its Néron special fiber is a one-dimensional k-torus. It is split precisely when the two tangent directions of the nodal minimal cubic are k-rational; otherwise the torus splits over the quadratic extension of k.
- Néron model and finite extension Weil restriction: Let S/R be a finite free extension of discrete valuation rings with fraction fields L/K. If 𝒜/S is the Néron model of an abelian variety A/L, then Res_(S/R)𝒜 exists and is the Néron model over R of Res_(L/K)A.
- Néron–Ogg–Shafarevich criterion: For an abelian variety A over a nonarchimedean local field K and a prime ℓ different from the residue characteristic, A has good reduction if and only if inertia acts trivially on T_ℓA. If this holds for one such ℓ it holds for every such ℓ.
- Q-curves from rational Atkin–Lehner quotient points: For squarefree N and P∈Y*(N)(Q), every geometric lift of P toY₀(N) represents an elliptic curve geometrically isogenous to all its Galois conjugates, hence aQ-curve. All curves represented by this fiber are geometrically isogenous. This asserts the forward construction, not the converse classification of allQ-curves.
- Rank-one rational Hom space for non-CM isogenous elliptic curves: If E,E′ are geometrically non-CM elliptic curves over a characteristic-zero field k and µ:E→E′ is a k-isogeny, then Hom_k(E,E′)⊗ℚ=ℚµ and End⁰_k(E)=ℚ. Every nonzero homomorphism E→E′ is an isogeny.
- Real periods and components of a real elliptic curve: For E/R and a nonzero real invariant differentialω, its complex period lattice has a unique primitive positive real periodω₁ and a basisω₁,ω₂ withImω₂>0 andReω₂ either0 orω₁/2. In the first caseE(R) has two connected components and all ofE[2] is real; in the second it is connected and only one nonzero2-torsion point is real.
- Regulator of an elliptic curve over a number field: For a ℤ-basis P₁,…,P_r of E(K)/E(K)_tors, the regulator is Reg(E/K)=det(⟨P_i,P_j⟩_K), using the specified relative Néron–Tate pairing. The rank-zero determinant is1. A basis change in GL_r(ℤ) does not alter it; torsion factors are not incorporated into this regulator.
- Root number of a weight-two newform: For a normalized weight-two newform f of primitive level N, its root number η_f is the unit-modulus complex constant in Λ(f,s)=η_fΛ(f*,2−s), where f* has conjugate Fourier coefficients and Λ(f,s)=N^(s/2)(2π)^(−s)Γ(s)L(f,s). If the coefficients of f are real, η_f is±1.
- Selmer group of an elliptic isogeny: For an isogenyφ:E→E′ over a number field F, Sel_φ(E/F) consists of classes c∈H¹(F,E[φ]) whose localization at every placev lies in the image of E′(F_v)/φE(F_v) under the local Kummer map. Forφ=[n] this is the n-Selmer group. The definition imposes local Kummer conditions, not local vanishing in H¹(F_v,E[φ]).
- Simple abelian variety of GL₂-type over ℚ: A ℚ-simple abelian variety A is of GL₂-type if End⁰_ℚ(A) is a number field of degree dim A over ℚ. This convention concerns simple varieties; the broader convention allowing a commutative semisimple subalgebra of dimension dim A also permits products.
- Smoothed approximate functional equation for a weight-two newform: Let f=∑a_nqⁿ be a normalized weight-two newform of level N, with functional-equation factorη_f, and put b_n=2πn/√N. Then Λ(f,s)=∑_(n≥1)a_nb_n^(−s)Γ(s,b_n)+η_f∑_(n≥1)ā_nb_n^(s−2)Γ(2−s,b_n). Both series and all s-derivatives converge locally uniformly, giving exponentially convergent evaluations of values and derivatives from coefficients andη_f.
- Specialization of inertia-fixed Tate modules to a Néron special fiber: For an abelian variety A over a nonarchimedean local field K with Néron model𝒜 and residue field k of characteristic p, and ℓ≠p, reduction induces T_ℓ(A)^I_K≅T_ℓ(𝒜_k), equivariantly for the residue-field Galois group G_K/I_K. The whole special fiber may be used; its finite component group contributes no Tate module.
- Strongly modular abelian variety over a number field: An abelian variety A/K is strongly modular over K if its Hasse–Weil L-function is a finite product of L-functions of normalized weight-two newforms for groups Γ₁(N_i). The base field K is part of this property; geometric modularity alone does not imply it.
- Tate module of a Weil restriction: For a finite separable extension L/k of characteristic-zero fields and an abelian variety A/L, T_ℓ(Res_(L/k)A) is canonically isomorphic as a continuous G_k-module to Ind_(G_L)^(G_k)T_ℓ(A). Rationalizing gives the same identity for V_ℓ.
- Tate representation at multiplicative elliptic reduction: Let E/K have multiplicative reduction over a nonarchimedean local field with residue cardinality q, and ℓ≠char(k). On V=T_ℓE⊗Q_ℓ, inertia is nontrivial unipotent, V^I and V_I each have dimension one, and arithmetic Frobenius acts on them by εq and ε respectively, where ε=1 for split and−1 for nonsplit reduction. Thus the local Euler polynomial on the dual invariant representation is1−εT.
- Tate–Shafarevich group of an elliptic curve: For E over a number field F, Sha(E/F)=ker[H¹(F,E)→∏_vH¹(F_v,E)], with v running over all finite and infinite places. Each localization restricts cocycles using compatible algebraic-closure embeddings and includes the geometric points into E(F̄_v); its cohomology class is independent of these choices. Finiteness is not part of the definition.
- Twisting criterion for a completely defined quadratic Q-curve: LetK/Q be quadratic with a fixed liftν∈G_Q, letE/K be geometrically non-CM, and letµ:E→νE be a geometric isogeny. Writeσµ=c_σµ withc_σ∈{±1} forσ∈G_K. Forb∈K×, the twistE^b admits aK-defined isogeny toν(E^b) iff c_σ=κ_(N_K/Q b)(σ) for allσ∈G_K, whereκ_a(σ)=σ√a/√a.

INTRODUCED CONCEPTS (2)

- Bruin–Ferraguti construction of a quadratic Q-curve’s newform
- Bruin–Ferraguti rationality and denominator bound for quadratic Q-curves

LOCATED CONCEPTS (80)

1. Q-curve
   An elliptic curve over a number field is a ℚ-curve if it is isogenous over ℚ̄ to every Galois conjugate. It is completely defined over a Galois number field K if it and the isogenies between its conjugates are defined over K. A quadratic ℚ-curve is one defined over a quadratic number field.
   Source: relies_on, Definitions 3.1,5.1
   Note: Isogenies to conjugates and complete field of definition.

2. Normalized weight-two newform
   A normalized weight-two newform of level N and character χ is a simultaneous eigenform for all classical Hecke operators in the new cuspidal subspace, with first q-coefficient one. The new subspace is the Petersson-orthogonal complement of forms obtained as g(dτ) from lower levels dividing N.
   Source: relies_on, Notation; §§3–7
   Note: Normalized eigenforms with character and quadratic coefficient field.

3. Modular parametrization of an elliptic curve
   A modular parametrization over K is a nonconstant morphism of algebraic curves π:X₁(N)_K→E for some N; for a rational elliptic curve the source may be X₀(N). Its pullback carries invariant differentials on E to weight-two cusp differentials.
   Source: relies_on, §§2–3,7–8
   Note: Relates modular differentials to the elliptic periods.

4. Optimal modular parametrization
   For fixed source X₁(N), a modular parametrization π:X₁(N)→E is optimal if it is maximal for domination: π′ dominates π when π=φ∘π′ for an isogeny φ:E′→E over the base field, with pairs considered up to isomorphism. The analogous X₀(N) notion is called strong in §2.
   Source: relies_on, §§2,8
   Note: Allows the Manin-ideal hypothesis.

5. Manin ideal of a modular parametrization
   For π:X₁(N)→E/K, let D_E be the O_K-lattice of invariant differentials integral at every finite place. If P=π*H⁰(E,Ω¹) and P_int=P∩O_K[[q]]dq/q, the Manin ideal c(π) is defined by π*D_E=c(π)P_int. It is the ideal analogue of the Manin constant when the integral differential module need not be free.
   Source: relies_on, Definition 8.1
   Note: Integral differential comparison, credited to González–Lario.

6. Generalized Manin conjecture for Q-curves
   For an optimal modular parametrization π:X₁(N)→E of a ℚ-curve completely defined over its base number field, the Manin ideal c(π) is the unit ideal.
   Source: relies_on, Conjecture 8.4
   Note: The effective denominator is conditional on this earlier conjecture.

7. Newform factorization for a completely defined quadratic Q-curve
   Let E/K be a non-CM ℚ-curve completely defined over a quadratic field K, and let μ:E→νE satisfy νμ∘μ=[m]. If m is not a square, there is a normalized weight-two newform f of level N and coefficient field ℚ(√m), with its unique Galois conjugate σf, such that L(E/K,s)=L(f,s)L(σf,s). If m is square, E is K-isogenous to a curve E₀/ℚ and the factorization is L(E₀,s)L(E₀^d,s), where K=ℚ(√d).
   Source: relies_on, §§3–6
   Note: Imported modularity and strong modularity yield the newform pair.

8. Manin–Drinfel’d theorem on cuspidal divisor classes
   For a compact modular curve associated to a congruence subgroup, every degree-zero divisor supported on its cusps has torsion class in its Jacobian. In particular a modular parametrization sends the difference of two cusps to a torsion point on an elliptic curve.
   Source: relies_on, Theorem 2.3; §7
   Note: Turns cusp paths into integral period cycles after torsion multiplication.

9. Complex uniformization theorem for an elliptic curve
   For an elliptic curve E over ℂ with nonzero invariant differential ω, there is a full lattice Λ⊆ℂ and a complex analytic group isomorphism ℂ/Λ≅E(ℂ) pulling ω back to dz. The lattice is the set of integrals of ω over integral closed cycles.
   Source: relies_on, §§2,5,7
   Note: Period-lattice interpretation.

10. Weierstrass ideal of an elliptic differential
   For an elliptic curve E/K and invariant differential ω, choose a local minimal differential ω_𝔭 at every finite prime. The Weierstrass ideal is δ_ω=∏_𝔭 𝔭^(v_𝔭(ω/ω_𝔭)), a fractional ideal independent of the unit choices. It can be nonprincipal, and a global minimal differential need not exist.
   Source: relies_on, §§8–10
   Note: Handles nonfree global integral differentials.

11. Cohomology of a group with abelian coefficients
   For a G-module M, Hⁿ(G,M)=ker(d:Cⁿ→C^(n+1))/im(d:C^(n−1)→Cⁿ), where Cⁿ consists of maps Gⁿ→M and (df)(g₁,…,g_(n+1))=g₁f(g₂,…)+∑_(i=1)^n(−1)^if(…,g_ig_(i+1),…)+(−1)^(n+1)f(g₁,…,g_n). For a profinite G and a discrete M, use continuous cochains.
   Source: relies_on, §§3–4
   Note: Splitting maps for the Q-curve and inner-twist cocycles.

12. Dirichlet character
   A group character of (Z/qZ)× with complex values, extended periodically to Z by zero on integers not coprime to q. A character is primitive when it is not induced from a proper divisor of q.
   Source: relies_on, §§4,6–7
   Note: Inner twists, Nebentypus and quadratic Gauss sums.

13. Manin ideal of a modular parametrization
   For π:X₁(N)→E/K, let D_E be the O_K-lattice of invariant differentials integral at every finite place. If P=π*H⁰(E,Ω¹) and P_int=P∩O_K[[q]]dq/q, the Manin ideal c(π) is defined by π*D_E=c(π)P_int. It is the ideal analogue of the Manin constant when the integral differential module need not be free.
   Source: explains, Definition 8.1
   Note: Prior definitions/conjectures recalled with their cited provenance.

14. Generalized Manin conjecture for Q-curves
   For an optimal modular parametrization π:X₁(N)→E of a ℚ-curve completely defined over its base number field, the Manin ideal c(π) is the unit ideal.
   Source: explains, Conjecture 8.4
   Note: Prior definitions/conjectures recalled with their cited provenance.

15. Weierstrass ideal of an elliptic differential
   For an elliptic curve E/K and invariant differential ω, choose a local minimal differential ω_𝔭 at every finite prime. The Weierstrass ideal is δ_ω=∏_𝔭 𝔭^(v_𝔭(ω/ω_𝔭)), a fractional ideal independent of the unit choices. It can be nonprincipal, and a global minimal differential need not exist.
   Source: explains, §8
   Note: Prior definitions/conjectures recalled with their cited provenance.

16. Q-curve
   An elliptic curve over a number field is a ℚ-curve if it is isogenous over ℚ̄ to every Galois conjugate. It is completely defined over a Galois number field K if it and the isogenies between its conjugates are defined over K. A quadratic ℚ-curve is one defined over a quadratic number field.
   Source: explains, Definition 3.1
   Note: Prior definitions/conjectures recalled with their cited provenance.

17. Bruin–Ferraguti construction of a quadratic Q-curve’s newform
   Given a completely defined non-CM quadratic ℚ-curve E/K and a K-isogeny μ:E→νE with νμ∘μ=[m], m nonsquare, choose an embedding ℚ(√m)→ℂ. Compute the newform’s level from N²=N_K(E)norm·Δ_K², determine its character from the sign of m and the quadratic character of K, and match its Euler factors to those of E. At ambiguous primes the chosen μ distinguishes the sign via Frobenius and the reduced isogeny; this yields f rather than just the unordered conjugate pair.
   Source: introduces, §6
   Note: Constructive recovery starts with E and μ, rather than starting with the modular form.
   Origin evidence: The abstract and §1 explicitly claim the algorithm. §6 compares it with Cremona [11], explaining the reversed input/output direction and absence of the earlier prime-level restriction. The full sign-selection and bad-prime algorithms were inspected; the older modularity theorem is not reattributed.

18. Bruin–Ferraguti rationality and denominator bound for quadratic Q-curves
   Let E/K be a non-CM ℚ-curve completely defined over a quadratic field, not K-isogenous to a curve over ℚ, with μ:E→νE and νμ∘μ=[m]. Let μ*(ωνE)=αω_E, α=p+q√Δ_K of norm m, and D_α={x∈O_K:xα∈O_K}. Put R=L(E/K,1)√|Δ_K|/Ω_E. Then R∈ℚ. Assuming the generalized Manin conjecture, 2q·|E(K)_tors|²·t·N(D_α)·s²·R∈ℤ, where t=4 if m≡1 mod4 and t=1 otherwise, and s is the lcm of minimal K-isogeny degrees from all curves in E’s K-isogeny class to E.
   Source: introduces, Theorem 10.2; §§7–9
   Note: Rationality and the conditional effective denominator bound are the main new period result.
   Origin evidence: The abstract and §1 explicitly describe the new effective L-ratio result. §§7–9 identify the original period/denominator argument while §8 credits the Manin ideal and generalized conjecture to González–Lario [26]. The final theorem’s lcm convention was checked separately from the inconsistent gcd prose in §§2 and9.2.

19. Abelian variety
   An abelian variety over a field k is a smooth proper geometrically connected commutative group scheme over k. Geometric connectedness means connectedness after base change to an algebraic closure; its group law and identity are defined over k.
   Source: relies_on, §§3–7
   Note: The source imports modular abelian varieties, Weil restrictions and building blocks; this nonaffine group object cannot be replaced by the catalogue’s affine algebraic-group vocabulary.

20. Jacobian of a pointed smooth projective curve
   For a smooth projective geometrically connected curve C/k with P∈C(k), its Jacobian is an abelian variety J with a morphism ι_P:C→J sending P to0 such that every morphism C→A into an abelian variety sending P to0 factors uniquely through a homomorphism J→A. The underlying J is canonically independent of the chosen base point.
   Source: relies_on, §§2,4,7–8
   Note: Actual Jacobian/Abel maps used to construct A_f and the modular parametrization. On the chosen arithmetic modular model a rational base cusp is specified; general geometric divisor arguments can use any geometric base cusp.

21. Modular abelian variety attached to a weight-two newform
   For a normalized weight-two newform f on Γ₁(N), let I_f be the kernel of its eigenvalue homomorphism on the Hecke algebra T. The modular abelian variety A_f is the quotient J₁(N)/(I_fJ₁(N)), where I_fJ₁(N) is the abelian subvariety generated by the images of the operators in I_f. It is defined over ℚ and its complex differential space pulls back to the span of all Galois conjugates of f.
   Source: relies_on, §§3–7
   Note: The actual argument imports A_f as a modular Jacobian quotient, its differential space and Hecke coefficient action.

22. Strongly modular abelian variety over a number field
   An abelian variety A/K is strongly modular over K if its Hasse–Weil L-function is a finite product of L-functions of normalized weight-two newforms for groups Γ₁(N_i). The base field K is part of this property; geometric modularity alone does not imply it.
   Source: relies_on, Definition3.3,Theorem3.5,§5
   Note: Source distinguishes geometric modularity from a factorization of the L-function over its actual field.

23. Weil restriction of an abelian variety
   For a finite separable field extension L/k and an abelian variety A/L, Res_(L/k)A is the abelian variety over k representing the functor T↦Hom_L(T×_k L,A) on k-schemes. It has dimension [L:k]dim A; after a separable splitting extension it becomes the product of the conjugate abelian varieties.
   Source: relies_on, §§3,5,6
   Note: The proper abelian surface Res_(K/ℚ)E and its base change to E×νE are actual inputs; affine-only restriction vocabulary is insufficient.

24. Simple abelian variety of GL₂-type over ℚ
   A ℚ-simple abelian variety A is of GL₂-type if End⁰_ℚ(A) is a number field of degree dim A over ℚ. This convention concerns simple varieties; the broader convention allowing a commutative semisimple subalgebra of dimension dim A also permits products.
   Source: relies_on, §§3–6
   Note: Identifies the rational endomorphism field and modular abelian variety of the simple Weil restriction.

25. Tate module of an abelian variety
   For an abelian variety A over a characteristic-zero field k and a prime ℓ, T_ℓA=lim←A[ℓⁿ](k̄) with multiplication-by-ℓ transition maps. It is a free ℤ_ℓ-module of rank2dim A with its continuous absolute Galois action; V_ℓA=T_ℓA⊗_(ℤ_ℓ)ℚ_ℓ.
   Source: relies_on, §6.4(6.6)–(6.7)
   Note: Actual induced Tate modules, reduction invariants and coefficient-field action on B require the abelian-variety version.

26. Inner twist of a non-CM weight-two newform
   For a non-CM normalized weight-two newform f with coefficient field F, an inner twist is a pair(γ,χ) with γ:F→ℂ an embedding and χ a Dirichlet character satisfying γ(a_p(f))=χ(p)a_p(f) for almost all primes p. For such f the character is unique for a given γ, and the occurring embeddings are automorphisms of F.
   Source: relies_on, §4pp9–10;§6pp11–12
   Note: Actual conjugate newform/Dirichlet-character data used to find the splitting field and character.

27. Splitting field determined by newform inner twists
   For a non-CM weight-two newform, take the finite collection of its inner-twist characters χ. A character modulo M is viewed on Gal(ℚ(ζ_M)/ℚ) through a↦(ζ_M↦ζ_M^a), and determines the fixed field of its kernel. The splitting field attached to the newform is the compositum of these fields inside ℚ̄.
   Source: relies_on, Definition4.1 and§6
   Note: Actual field associated to all inner twists, later identified with K by imported endomorphism results.

28. Splitting map for a multiplicative Galois 2-cocycle
   Let a finite Galois group G act on a field F, and let c:G×G→F× be a multiplicative2-cocycle. A splitting map is a1-cochain β:G→F× such that c(σ,τ)=β(σ)σ(β(τ))/β(στ). Such a map exists exactly when the cohomology class of c is zero; two splitting maps differ by a1-cocycle.
   Source: relies_on, §4p10 and§7.1
   Note: Actual trivialization of the multiplicative cocycle; Hilbert90 and specific building-block differential facts remain imported inputs to record.

29. Building block of a modular abelian variety
   For a modular abelian variety A_f and a field L over which all its geometric endomorphisms are defined, a building block is an absolutely simple abelian variety B/L such that (A_f)_L is L-isogenous to a power of B. Its isogeny class, rather than a chosen equation or embedding into A_f, is intrinsic.
   Source: relies_on, §4Theorem4.2 and§7.1
   Note: Actual absolutely simple factors of A_f; in this source they are elliptic curves.

30. Invariant differentials on an abelian variety
   For an abelian variety A/k, an invariant differential is a global section of Ω¹_(A/k) unchanged by every translation after field extension. These form a k-vector space of dimension dim A, identified with the cotangent space at the identity by evaluation; homomorphisms pull them back to invariant differentials.
   Source: relies_on, §4pp9–10 and§7pp19–20
   Note: Actual differential-space identification and pullbacks from A_f to its elliptic building block.

31. Néron model of an abelian variety
   For an abelian variety A/K over the fraction field of a discrete valuation ring R, its Néron model is a smooth separated R-scheme 𝒜 of finite type with generic fiber A such that Hom_R(Z,𝒜)→Hom_K(Z_K,A) is bijective for every smooth R-scheme Z. Its commutative group-scheme structure is the unique extension of that of A.
   Source: relies_on, §6.4Lemma6.2,§8
   Note: Actual extension of isogenies/integral differentials and ramified restriction-of-scalars fibers uses the full Néron mapping property.

32. Good reduction of an abelian variety
   An abelian variety over the fraction field K of a discrete valuation ring R has good reduction if it extends to an abelian scheme over R. Equivalently its Néron model is proper over R; for elliptic curves this agrees with smooth reduction of a minimal Weierstrass model.
   Source: relies_on, §6pp12–18
   Note: Actual good/bad reduction for the Weil-restriction abelian surface, beyond the existing elliptic Weierstrass reduction definition.

33. Identity component and component group of a Néron special fiber
   For a Néron model 𝒜 over a discrete valuation ring with residue field k, the geometric identity component 𝒜⁰_(k̄) is the connected component containing the identity in its geometric special fiber. The geometric component group Φ(k̄)=𝒜_k(k̄)/𝒜⁰_k(k̄) is the finite group of connected components, with its natural Galois action. The open subgroup 𝒜⁰ has the whole generic fiber and only the identity component of the special fiber.
   Source: relies_on, §6.4Lemma6.2 andinertmultiplicativecase
   Note: Actual special-fiber identity groups and components in the sign computations.

34. Zariski tangent and cotangent space at a rational point
   For a k-rational point x of a k-scheme X, its cotangent space is 𝔪_x/𝔪_x² over k, where 𝔪_x is the maximal ideal of O_(X,x). Its dual Hom_k(𝔪_x/𝔪_x²,k) is the Zariski tangent space T_xX; equivalently tangent vectors are k-morphisms Spec k[ε]/(ε²)→X reducing to x.
   Source: relies_on, Lemma6.2proofp17
   Note: Actual tangent space and square-zero ramification quotient in the Greenberg filtration; it is not a differential-geometric surface tangent space.

35. Conductor ideal of an abelian variety over a number field
   For an abelian variety A/K over a number field, its conductor ideal is 𝔑_K(A)=∏_v𝔭_v^(a(V_ℓA|_(G_Kv))), with ℓ different from the residue characteristic at v. The exponent uses the inertia-fixed codimension and Swan conductor of its rational Tate representation and is independent of ℓ; only finitely many exponents are nonzero.
   Source: relies_on, §6.1(6.3),Lemma6.1
   Note: Actual conductor ideal of E and its Weil restriction, with precise local ℓ-adic/Swan convention.

36. Relative discriminant ideal of number fields
   For a finite extension L/K of number fields, the relative discriminant 𝔡_(L/K) is the O_K-ideal locally generated by determinants det(Tr_(L/K)(b_i b_j)) for integral bases after localization at primes of O_K. For K=ℚ it is generated by the ordinary field discriminant up to sign.
   Source: relies_on, §6.1(6.3)
   Note: Actual discriminant factor in the conductor-induction/level formula.

37. Relative ideal norm of number fields
   For a finite extension L/K of number fields, the ideal norm sends a prime 𝔓 of O_L above 𝔭 of O_K to 𝔭^[O_L/𝔓:O_K/𝔭] and extends multiplicatively to nonzero fractional ideals. For K=ℚ its positive generator is the absolute ideal norm.
   Source: relies_on, §6.1(6.3)
   Note: Actual norm of the K-conductor ideal to ℚ, beyond its absolute-number notation.

38. Arithmetic modular curve with µ_N level structure
   For N≥4 over ℚ, Y₁^µ(N) represents isomorphism classes of elliptic-curve families with a closed group-scheme embedding µ_N↪E[N]; X₁^µ(N) is its smooth projective compactification. Its complex point τ corresponds to E_τ=ℂ/(ℤ+τℤ), with ζ_N↦1/N. This fixes the arithmetic model and complex identification: its cusp∞ is ℚ-rational and its local parameter is q=exp(2πiτ).
   Source: relies_on, §7.1Theorem7.2/Lemma7.3 and§4
   Note: The cusp-action formula and rational∞ use the dualµ_N model, not the marked-point model with the same naive complex parameter. PrimaryHida and officialSage explicitly match this convention.

39. Conductor formula for Weil restriction of an abelian variety
   For an abelian variety A/L of dimension g and a finite extension of number fields L/K, 𝔑_K(Res_(L/K)A)=Norm_(L/K)(𝔑_L(A))·𝔡_(L/K)^(2g). In particular for an elliptic curve over quadratic K/ℚ the rational conductor is the absolute norm of its K-conductor times Δ_K².
   Source: relies_on, §6.1(6.3)
   Note: Actual imported Milne conductor formula used to determine the level.

40. Conductor of the abelian variety attached to a newform
   For a normalized weight-two newform f of level N and coefficient field F, its associated abelian variety A_f/ℚ has conductor N^[F:ℚ]. The integer N is the primitive newform level, not a chosen oldform ambient level.
   Source: relies_on, §6.1(6.3)
   Note: Actual primitive-newform conductor/level fact used in the N² equation.

41. Tate module of a Weil restriction
   For a finite separable extension L/k of characteristic-zero fields and an abelian variety A/L, T_ℓ(Res_(L/k)A) is canonically isomorphic as a continuous G_k-module to Ind_(G_L)^(G_k)T_ℓ(A). Rationalizing gives the same identity for V_ℓ.
   Source: relies_on, §6.4(6.6)
   Note: Actual induced Tate-module identity in the coefficient-sign argument.

42. Hilbert’s theorem90
   For a field F with separable closure Fsep, H¹(Gal(Fsep/F),(Fsep)×)=0. Equivalently, for a finite Galois extension L/F every multiplicative1-cocycle c_σ is b⁻¹σ(b) for some b∈L×.
   Source: relies_on, §4p10,changesofsplittingmap
   Note: The source uses β′(σ)=β(σ)σ(a)/a for two splittings over Gal(F/ℚ). This is the actual Hilbert90 implication, independent of the general1-cocycle difference statement.

43. Néron model and finite extension Weil restriction
   Let S/R be a finite free extension of discrete valuation rings with fraction fields L/K. If 𝒜/S is the Néron model of an abelian variety A/L, then Res_(S/R)𝒜 exists and is the Néron model over R of Res_(L/K)A.
   Source: relies_on, Lemma6.2p17
   Note: Actual comparison of the Weil restriction of the integral model with the Néron model of B.

44. Greenberg filtration of a smooth group over a truncated DVR
   Let 𝒢 be a smooth commutative group scheme over a DVR S with residue field k and maximal ideal 𝔪, and suppose the k-algebra S/𝔪^e is identified with k[t]/(t^e). Assume the indicated Weil restrictions are representable. In X=Res_((S/𝔪^e)/k)(𝒢_(S/𝔪^e)), let F⁰X=X and F^iX be the kernel of reduction to 𝒢 modulo t^i for1≤i≤e. Then Gr⁰X≅𝒢_k and Gr^iX≅Lie(𝒢_k)⊗_k(𝔪/𝔪²)^(⊗i) for1≤i<e, as additive vector groups.
   Source: relies_on, Lemma6.2p17
   Note: Actual graded-fiber calculation: in ramifiedquadraticgoodreduction case S/pS≅Fp[t]/t² and the tangent kernel isGa. The source finalGr⁰=Ga line is a Gr¹ typo; primary§5.1 separates the two.

45. Elliptic building blocks from inner-twist splitting maps
   Let f be a non-CM weight-two newform with abelian coefficient field F, whose inner twists range over Gal(F/ℚ), and suppose its Gauss-sum cocycle c(σ,τ)=g(χ_σ⁻¹)g(σχ_τ⁻¹)/g(χ_(στ)⁻¹) is the coboundary of β:Gal(F/ℚ)→F×. Over the inner-twist splitting field L, there is w_β∈End_L(A_f) whose image is an elliptic building block and whose differential line pulls back to the span of ∑_σ[g(χ_σ⁻¹)/β(σ)]σf. All elliptic building blocks arise by applying coefficient-field endomorphisms to this image.
   Source: relies_on, Theorem4.2;§7changeofdifferentialbasis
   Note: Actual imported González–Lario endomorphism/differential result. It is not a Bruin–Ferraguti original contribution.

46. Integrality of the Manin ideal
   For a modular parametrization π:X₁^µ(N)→E of a completely defined non-CM ℚ-curve over a number field K, with q-expansion at the rational cusp of the µ_N arithmetic model, its Manin ideal c(π) is an integral O_K-ideal.
   Source: relies_on, Theorem8.3 and§8.1
   Note: Actual imported integrality, separate from the generalizedManin conjecture; proof unavailable as documented.

47. Cyclotomic Galois action on µ_N-level cusps
   On the µ_N arithmetic model of X₁(N), and its quotients associated to Γ₁(N)⊆Γ⊆Γ₀(N), every cusp is defined over ℚ(ζ_N). If σ_d(ζ_N)=ζ_N^d, it sends the cusp with primitive residue pair(x,y) modulo N to the cusp represented by any primitive integer lift of(x,d⁻¹y) modulo N. The result is read as a cusp residue class, not by cancelling a nonprimitive fraction; in particular∞ is rational.
   Source: relies_on, Theorem7.2/Lemma7.3
   Note: Actual field-of-definition input to multiplying cusp differences by |E(K)_tors|. Primitive residue-pair convention is explicit.

48. Analytic rank of an elliptic curve
   For an elliptic curve E over a number field K whose Hasse–Weil L-function is holomorphic near s=1, its analytic rank is ord_(s=1)L(E/K,s). The definition is conditional on the needed continuation; it does not assert general modularity or equality with the Mordell–Weil rank.
   Source: relies_on, §10.1/§11
   Note: Actual analytic rank terminology, with its analytic-continuation qualification explicit.

49. Root number of a weight-two newform
   For a normalized weight-two newform f of primitive level N, its root number η_f is the unit-modulus complex constant in Λ(f,s)=η_fΛ(f*,2−s), where f* has conjugate Fourier coefficients and Λ(f,s)=N^(s/2)(2π)^(−s)Γ(s)L(f,s). If the coefficients of f are real, η_f is±1.
   Source: relies_on, §11(11.1)andexamples
   Note: Actual complex functional-equation sign; it need not be±1 for imaginary coefficient fields.

50. Regulator of an elliptic curve over a number field
   For a ℤ-basis P₁,…,P_r of E(K)/E(K)_tors, the regulator is Reg(E/K)=det(⟨P_i,P_j⟩_K), using the specified relative Néron–Tate pairing. The rank-zero determinant is1. A basis change in GL_r(ℤ) does not alter it; torsion factors are not incorporated into this regulator.
   Source: relies_on, §10.1p29
   Note: Actual BSD regulator definition; paired with relative height convention and separate torsion denominator.

51. Local Tamagawa number of an elliptic curve
   For E over a nonarchimedean local field K with finite residue field k, let E⁰(K) be the points reducing into the identity component of its Néron special fiber. The local Tamagawa number is c_K=[E(K):E⁰(K)]=|Φ(k)|, where Φ is the component group. It is1 at good-reduction places.
   Source: relies_on, §10.1p30
   Note: Actual finite-place component index in the period formula.

52. Atkin–Lehner involution on X₀(N)
   For an exact divisor Q|N with gcd(Q,N/Q)=1, the Atkin–Lehner involution w_Q on X₀(N) sends(E,C) to(E/C_Q,C′), where C_Q⊆C has order Q and C′ is generated by the kernel E[Q]/C_Q of the dual quotient isogeny and the image of the order-N/Q subgroup of C. It extends to the compact curve overℚ; for squarefreeN these involutions generate an elementary abelian2-group.
   Source: relies_on, §11p31
   Note: Actual standard involutions and quotient method used to obtain Qcurve examples, not original to this source.

53. Mordell–Weil rank of an elliptic curve
   For an elliptic curve E over a number field F, its Mordell–Weil rank is dim_ℚ(ℚ⊗_ℤE(F)), equivalently the number of infinite cyclic factors in E(F) when finite generation is known. It concerns rational points and is distinct from analytic rank defined by an L-function zero.
   Source: relies_on, §10.1/§11
   Note: Actual algebraic rank terminology; exact NT canonical definition reused.

54. Tate–Shafarevich group of an elliptic curve
   For E over a number field F, Sha(E/F)=ker[H¹(F,E)→∏_vH¹(F_v,E)], with v running over all finite and infinite places. Each localization restricts cocycles using compatible algebraic-closure embeddings and includes the geometric points into E(F̄_v); its cohomology class is independent of these choices. Finiteness is not part of the definition.
   Source: relies_on, §10.1p29
   Note: Actual local-global kernel in BSD discussion; no finiteness inferred.

55. Selmer group of an elliptic isogeny
   For an isogenyφ:E→E′ over a number field F, Sel_φ(E/F) consists of classes c∈H¹(F,E[φ]) whose localization at every placev lies in the image of E′(F_v)/φE(F_v) under the local Kummer map. Forφ=[n] this is the n-Selmer group. The definition imposes local Kummer conditions, not local vanishing in H¹(F_v,E[φ]).
   Source: relies_on, §11 rankupperbounds via[53]
   Note: Actual general isogeny/multiplication descent background in the cited rankalgorithm; existingNTcanonicaldefinition reused.

56. Mordell–Weil theorem for elliptic curves
   For an elliptic curve E over a number field F, E(F) is a finitely generated abelian group, hence is isomorphic to E(F)_tors⊕ℤʳ for a finite nonnegative integerr.
   Source: relies_on, §10.1regulatorbasis/§11
   Note: Actual finite generation allowing a torsion-free finitebasis. NT owns the pending immediateproof review; itsfalseflag staysvisible.

57. BSD period of an elliptic curve over a number field
   For E/K choose a nonzero invariant differential ω and additive Haar measures dx_v whose product on𝔄_K gives vol(𝔄_K/K)=1. Its BSD period is P(E/K)=∏_(v finite)[L_v(E,1)∫_(E(K_v))|ω|_v]·∏_(v infinite)∫_(E(K_v))|ω|_v. Almost all finite factors are1; the product formula makes the result independent of ω, and the adelic normalization fixes its discriminant factor.
   Source: relies_on, §10.1p29–30
   Note: Actual period invariant compared to Ω_E/√|Δ_K|, with adelic measure convention and convergence factors.

58. Néron local volume formula for an elliptic curve
   Let E/K have a minimal integral Weierstrass model over a nonarchimedean local field with residue size q, let ω_min be a minimal invariant differential and normalize additive Haar measure by vol(O_K)=1. Then ∫_(E(K))|ω_min|=c_K/L_K(E,1), where c_K=[E(K):E⁰(K)] and L_K is the local Hasse–Weil Euler factor.
   Source: relies_on, §10.1(10.1)
   Note: Actual finite-place calculation linking local periods and Tamagawa factors; fullGrossproofread.

59. Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer rank conjecture over a number field
   For an elliptic curve E over a number field K, its Hasse–Weil L-function is conjectured to have analytic continuation to an entire function, with ord_(s=1)L(E/K,s) equal to its Mordell–Weil rank. No general continuation or rank equality is asserted by this conjecture record.
   Source: explains, Conjecture10.3p29
   Note: Recalled standard conjecture, not assumed by the main theorem and not original to this source.

60. Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer leading-term conjecture over a number field
   For E/K of Mordell–Weil rank r, the BSD conjecture asserts analytic continuation and analytic rank r, finiteness of Sha(E/K), and lim_(s→1)L(E/K,s)/(s−1)^r=P(E/K)Reg(E/K)|Sha(E/K)|/|E(K)_tors|². Here P is the adelically normalized BSD period and Reg is the relative height-pairing determinant on a torsion-free basis, with torsion kept separate.
   Source: explains, Conjecture10.4p30
   Note: Recalled standard leading-term conjecture; explanatory comparison only, not a proved result or original contribution.

61. Specialization of inertia-fixed Tate modules to a Néron special fiber
   For an abelian variety A over a nonarchimedean local field K with Néron model𝒜 and residue field k of characteristic p, and ℓ≠p, reduction induces T_ℓ(A)^I_K≅T_ℓ(𝒜_k), equivariantly for the residue-field Galois group G_K/I_K. The whole special fiber may be used; its finite component group contributes no Tate module.
   Source: relies_on, §6.4(6.7)p16 andramifiedcasepp17–18
   Note: Actual equivariant comparison of inertia-fixed Tate modules with specialfiber used for coefficientsigns.

62. Néron–Ogg–Shafarevich criterion
   For an abelian variety A over a nonarchimedean local field K and a prime ℓ different from the residue characteristic, A has good reduction if and only if inertia acts trivially on T_ℓA. If this holds for one such ℓ it holds for every such ℓ.
   Source: relies_on, §6.1–6.4
   Note: Actual good/bad-reduction versus inertia assertion used with the conductor and localfactor calculations.

63. Néron identity fiber in multiplicative elliptic reduction
   For an elliptic curve E over a nonarchimedean local field K with residue field k, multiplicative reduction means that the identity component of its Néron special fiber is a one-dimensional k-torus. It is split precisely when the two tangent directions of the nodal minimal cubic are k-rational; otherwise the torus splits over the quadratic extension of k.
   Source: relies_on, §6.4p18
   Note: Actual identification of the nonsplit torus smooth reduction used for the sign algorithm.

64. Tate representation at multiplicative elliptic reduction
   Let E/K have multiplicative reduction over a nonarchimedean local field with residue cardinality q, and ℓ≠char(k). On V=T_ℓE⊗Q_ℓ, inertia is nontrivial unipotent, V^I and V_I each have dimension one, and arithmetic Frobenius acts on them by εq and ε respectively, where ε=1 for split and−1 for nonsplit reduction. Thus the local Euler polynomial on the dual invariant representation is1−εT.
   Source: relies_on, §6.4p18
   Note: Actual unipotent inertia, invariant/coinvariant dimensions and Frobenius signs used in the inert multiplicative case.

65. Gauss sum of a primitive quadratic character
   For a fundamental discriminant D and χ_D, ∑_(a mod|D|)χ_D(a)exp(2πina/|D|)=χ_D(n)√D for every positive n, where√D is the positive real square root when D>0 and i√|D| when D<0. In particular the Gauss sum at n=1 has this exact value.
   Source: relies_on, §7p19 beforeκdefinition
   Note: Actual exact value g(χ)=√Δ_K used for the basis change and splitting-map formulas; the sign convention is part of the result.

66. Mellin formula for a weight-two newform
   For a normalized weight-two newform f of level N, Λ(f,s)=N^(s/2)(2π)^(-s)Γ(s)L(f,s)=N^(s/2)∫₀^∞f(it)t^(s−1)dt. The integral converges locally uniformly for all complex s and gives an entire continuation; at s=1 it gives L(f,1)=−2πi∫₀^(i∞)f(z)dz.
   Source: relies_on, §7(7.1)p19
   Note: Actual central-value/period-integral relation; extends by linearity to the paper’s h.

67. Functional equation of a weight-two newform
   For a normalized weight-two newform f of level N, its completed entire L-function Λ(f,s)=N^(s/2)(2π)^(-s)Γ(s)L(f,s) satisfies Λ(f,s)=η_fΛ(f*,2−s), where f* has conjugate Fourier coefficients, η_f is algebraic with|η_f|=1, and η_f=−w_f in the convention W_Nf=w_ff*. If all coefficients are real thenη_f∈{±1}.
   Source: relies_on, §11(11.1)p32
   Note: Actual completed L-function equation and complex root factor used in central-value/rank arguments.

68. Smoothed approximate functional equation for a weight-two newform
   Let f=∑a_nqⁿ be a normalized weight-two newform of level N, with functional-equation factorη_f, and put b_n=2πn/√N. Then Λ(f,s)=∑_(n≥1)a_nb_n^(−s)Γ(s,b_n)+η_f∑_(n≥1)ā_nb_n^(s−2)Γ(2−s,b_n). Both series and all s-derivatives converge locally uniformly, giving exponentially convergent evaluations of values and derivatives from coefficients andη_f.
   Source: relies_on, §11pp32–37 citingDokchitser[17]
   Note: Actual numerical evaluation/derivative method used through Sage; this prior method is not an original contribution of the assigned source.

69. Determinant of the Tate representation of a weight-two newform
   For a normalized weight-two newform f of level N and nebentypusχ, V_(f,λ) has dimension two over F_λ and detρ_(f,λ)=χ·χ_ℓ as characters of G_Q, whereχ is viewed as a Galois character andχ_ℓ is the ℓ-adic cyclotomic character. This character identity applies on every decomposition group, including primes dividing N.
   Source: relies_on, §6.1pp13–14 and§6.4p18
   Note: Actual two-dimensional coefficient-field Tate representation and its global determinant, used to deduce the second Frobenius eigenvalue at the inert multiplicative prime.

70. Q-curves from rational Atkin–Lehner quotient points
   For squarefree N and P∈Y*(N)(Q), every geometric lift of P toY₀(N) represents an elliptic curve geometrically isogenous to all its Galois conjugates, hence aQ-curve. All curves represented by this fiber are geometrically isogenous. This asserts the forward construction, not the converse classification of allQ-curves.
   Source: relies_on, §11p31
   Note: Actual recalled method for obtaining exampleQ-curves from rational quotient points, creditedtoElkies; only the forward construction is used.

71. Twisting criterion for a completely defined quadratic Q-curve
   LetK/Q be quadratic with a fixed liftν∈G_Q, letE/K be geometrically non-CM, and letµ:E→νE be a geometric isogeny. Writeσµ=c_σµ withc_σ∈{±1} forσ∈G_K. Forb∈K×, the twistE^b admits aK-defined isogeny toν(E^b) iff c_σ=κ_(N_K/Q b)(σ) for allσ∈G_K, whereκ_a(σ)=σ√a/√a.
   Source: relies_on, §11p31 citingHasegawa[31,Cor4.3]
   Note: Reusable general descent criterion underlying the completely-defined twists used for examples. Family coefficients remain notes; noHasegawa originality is reassigned.

72. Integrality of the X₀ Manin constant
   For a modular parametrizationπ:X₀(N)→E/Q at the conductor level and a global minimal differentialω_E, the signed Manin constant c(π) is a nonzero integer. In particular the unsigned constant of the optimal quotient is a positive integer.
   Source: relies_on, §2Theorem2.1p4 andequations2.4–2.5
   Note: Actual rational-curve geometric model uses integral Maninconstant; X₀ conductor-level scope is explicit.

73. Manin conjecture for an optimal X₀ elliptic quotient
   For an optimal modular parametrizationX₀(N)→E/Q at the conductor level, the unsigned Manin constant is1, or equivalently the signed constant attached to a chosen minimal differential is±1. This is a conjecture, not asserted by its definition or by integrality.
   Source: relies_on, §2Conjecture2.2p4
   Note: Actual conditional rational-curve comparison; separate from X₁/number-field generalizedManin conjecture and not a proved assumption.

74. Manin constant of an X₀ modular parametrization
   For E/Q of conductor N, let f be its normalized weight-two newform andπ:X₀(N)→E a modular parametrization. For a global minimal invariant differentialω_E, the nonzero rational number c(π) is defined byπ*ω_E=c(π)f(q)dq/q. Changing the sign ofω_E changes the sign ofc(π); its absolute value is the unsigned Manin constant.
   Source: explains, §2p4
   Note: Recalled classical definition, nooriginalattribution.

75. Manin conjecture for an optimal X₀ elliptic quotient
   For an optimal modular parametrizationX₀(N)→E/Q at the conductor level, the unsigned Manin constant is1, or equivalently the signed constant attached to a chosen minimal differential is±1. This is a conjecture, not asserted by its definition or by integrality.
   Source: explains, §2Conjecture2.2p4
   Note: ClassicalManin conjecture recalled; nooriginclaim.

76. Kato rank-zero criterion for a modular abelian quotient
   Let A/Q be an abelian variety admitting a surjective homomorphism J₁(N)→A for some N. If L(A/Q,1)≠0, then A(Q) is finite. The assertion is a theorem and does not assume the full Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture.
   Source: relies_on, §11p31 citing[32,Cor14.3]
   Note: Actual nonzero-L⇒rank0 modular-quotient input. Applied toRes_(K/Q)E, which isQ-isogenous to the appropriate modular quotient by the already recorded factorization and Weil-restriction identities; hence a nontorsionE(K) point forcesL(E/K,1)=0.

77. Finiteness of an elliptic isogeny class over a number field
   For an elliptic curve E over a number field K, only finitely many K-isomorphism classes of elliptic curves are K-isogenous toE.
   Source: relies_on, §9.2pp27–28
   Note: Actual finiteness of theK-isogeny class underlying the finite lcm/max of minimal degrees.

78. Effective prime-isogeny enumeration over a number field
   For a geometrically non-CM elliptic curve E/K over a number field, an algorithm determines every rational primep admitting aK-defined cyclicp-isogeny fromE and computes all such isogenies up to targetK-isomorphism.
   Source: relies_on, §9.2pp27–28 citing[4]
   Note: Actual effective prime-isogeny neighbor procedure; non-CM scope matches main setup.

79. Isogeny graph of a non-CM elliptic curve over a number field
   For a geometrically non-CM elliptic curve E/K, its K-isogeny graph has vertices the K-isomorphism classes K-isogenous toE and an edge labeledp between two vertices when there is aK-defined cyclic isogeny of prime degreep between them. Dual isogenies make the edges undirected; the graph is finite and connected, and traversing prime-degree isogenies enumerates its vertices.
   Source: relies_on, §9.2pp27–28
   Note: Actual graph construction used to obtain minimal isogeny degrees and the final lcm. Earlygcd claim remains an explicitly recorded sourceissue.

80. Integral basis of a quadratic number field
   Letd≠1 be a nonzero squarefree integer andF=Q(√d). ThenO_F=Z[√d] ifd≡2 or3 mod4, andO_F=Z[(1+√d)/2] ifd≡1 mod4. The squarefree assumption on the chosen radicand is essential; the same formulas with an arbitrary merely nonsquarem are not valid.
   Source: relies_on, §9.1p27
   Note: Actual classical input to the coefficient-denominator estimate, recorded with the essential squarefree hypothesis rather than silently validating its printed misuse.

81. Denominator ideal of an element of a number field
   Forα in a number field K, the denominator ideal isD_α={a∈O_K:aα∈O_K}. It is a nonzero integral ideal ofO_K and is the largest integral ideal whose product withα lies inO_K. This definition uses the full ring of integers rather than a chosen possibly nonmaximal order.
   Source: relies_on, §9.1p27/Theorem10.2
   Note: Actual denominator-ideal definition in the finalstatement.

82. Real periods and components of a real elliptic curve
   For E/R and a nonzero real invariant differentialω, its complex period lattice has a unique primitive positive real periodω₁ and a basisω₁,ω₂ withImω₂>0 andReω₂ either0 orω₁/2. In the first caseE(R) has two connected components and all ofE[2] is real; in the second it is connected and only one nonzero2-torsion point is real.
   Source: relies_on, Lemma7.4p22;§10.1p30
   Note: Actual real-period primitive generator and real-component/2-torsion factor used by the period comparison.

83. Rank-one rational Hom space for non-CM isogenous elliptic curves
   If E,E′ are geometrically non-CM elliptic curves over a characteristic-zero field k and µ:E→E′ is a k-isogeny, then Hom_k(E,E′)⊗ℚ=ℚµ and End⁰_k(E)=ℚ. Every nonzero homomorphism E→E′ is an isogeny.
   Source: relies_on, §5;Lemma7.1p20;§8.1p25
   Note: Actual scalar comparison ofisogenies and minimaldegree reductions. Previously only proof-onlysupport; now independently expanded because actually required bysource.

84. Inner twist and nebentypus of a quadratic Q-curve newform
   Let E/K be a geometrically non-CM completely defined quadraticQ-curve with conjugate isogenyµ andνµµ=[m], where m is nonsquare, and let f be the associated newform with coefficient fieldF=Q(√m). Its nontrivial coefficient conjugationσ satisfiesσ(a_p)=χ_K(p)a_p atp outside the level, whereχ_K is the primitive quadratic character ofK. The nebentypus of f is trivial when m>0 and isχ_K, viewed modulo the level, when m<0.
   Source: relies_on, §6pp11–12 beforelocalfactoralgorithm
   Note: Actual imported inner-twist/character identification behind the construction’s character step; its newform coefficient field and non-CM assumptions are explicit.

85. Integration pairing for weight-two modular symbols
   For f∈S₂(Γ₁(N)), the assignment{a,b}↦∫_a^b f(z)dz defines a well-defined complex-linear integration pairing withM₂(Γ₁(N))⊗C. The integrals at cusps converge, depend only on the modular-symbol class, and are additive in both the cusp form and the symbol.
   Source: relies_on, §2p5 and§7pp19–24
   Note: Actual meaning of{0,i∞}, cusp integrals and their period pushforward, separate from contextual performance comparison with modular-symbol algorithms.

ADDITIONAL PREREQUISITES (357)

- Abelian group [definition]: A group whose operation is commutative.
- Abelian group with a group action [definition]: A G-module is an abelian group M equipped with an action of G by abelian-group automorphisms. A morphism is a group homomorphism commuting with the G-actions.
- Abelian scheme [definition]: An abelian scheme over S is a proper smooth group scheme over S whose geometric fibers are connected. Each geometric fiber is an abelian variety.
- Abelian subvariety [definition]: An abelian subvariety of A/k is a closed subgroup scheme B⊆A which is itself an abelian variety over k, with the inclusion preserving the group structures.
- Abel–Jacobi map on curve divisors [construction]: For a pointed smooth projective curve C/k with Jacobian(J,ι_P), a geometric degree-zero divisor ∑n_QQ maps to ∑n_Qι_P(Q) in J(k̄). The map kills principal divisors and identifies degree-zero divisor classes with J(k̄), compatibly with the Galois action; it is independent of P on degree-zero divisors.
- Absolute Galois group [definition]: The group Gal(F^sep/F), with its Krull topology, where F^sep is a separable closure.
- Absolute norm of a fractional ideal [definition]: For a nonzero integral ideal I⊆O_K, N(I)=|O_K/I|. Extend this multiplicatively to nonzero fractional ideals by N(I/J)=N(I)/N(J); for a principal ideal (a), this equals |N_(K/ℚ)(a)|.
- Absolute value on a field [definition]: A map |·|:K→R_{≥0} that vanishes exactly at zero, is multiplicative and satisfies |x+y|≤|x|+|y|. It is nonarchimedean if the stronger bound |x+y|≤max(|x|,|y|) holds.
- Adele ring of a number field [definition]: The restricted product A_F=∏′_v F_v of all completions of a number field,where almost every finite component lies in the valuation ring O_v.
- Affine algebraic group over a field [definition]: An affine algebraic group over a field F is specified by a finitely generated commutative Hopf F-algebra A: for each commutative F-algebra R its group of R-points is Hom_(F-alg)(A,R), with multiplication induced by the coproduct. Equivalently it is a finite-type affine group scheme over F.
- Affine scheme [construction]: The affine scheme Spec R is the prime spectrum of R with the structure sheaf characterized on standard opens by O(D(f))=R_f and the localization restriction maps. Its stalk at 𝔭 is R_𝔭. An affine scheme is any locally ringed space isomorphic to one of these.
- Algebraic closure of a field [definition]: An algebraic extension of a field that is itself algebraically closed. This specifies an object, without including the existence or uniqueness-up-to-isomorphism theorem.
- Algebraic dual of a vector space [construction]: For a vector space V over a field k, its algebraic dual V* is Hom_k(V,k), with pointwise addition and scalar multiplication. No topology or continuity condition is imposed.
- Algebraic element of a field extension [definition]: An element α of L/F is algebraic over F if a nonzero polynomial in F[x] vanishes at α.
- Algebraic integer [definition]: A complex number satisfying a monic polynomial with integer coefficients.
- Algebraic torus [definition]: An algebraic group that becomes isomorphic over an algebraic closure to a finite product of copies of the multiplicative group.
- Algebraically closed field [definition]: A field in which every nonconstant polynomial has a root.
- Arithmetic modular curve X₀(N) [construction]: Over ℚ, Y₀(N) is the coarse moduli curve of elliptic curves with a cyclic subgroup scheme of order N; X₀(N) is its smooth projective compactification. On complex points τ corresponds to ℂ/(ℤ+τℤ) with subgroup generated by1/N, giving Γ₀(N)\ℍ. The arithmetic curve and its moduli convention are distinguished from the bare complex quotient.
- Artin and Swan conductor of a local ℓ-adic representation [construction]: For a local ℓ-adic representation V, its Swan exponent is Sw(V)=∫₀^∞codim_F V^(G_K^u)du, where G_K^u are the positive upper ramification groups. The Artin conductor exponent is a(V)=codim_F V^I_K+Sw(V); its conductor ideal is 𝔭_K^(a(V)). These exponents are nonnegative integers in this setting.
- Associative algebra over a field [definition]: A vector space with an associative bilinear multiplication and a multiplicative identity.
- Automorphism of a group [definition]: A bijective group homomorphism from a group to itself; automorphisms form a group under composition.
- Base change and fibers of a scheme [construction]: For X→S and S′→S, the base change is X_(S′)=X×_S S′ with its projection to S′. The fiber at s∈S is X_s=X×_S Spec κ(s), where κ(s)=O_(S,s)/𝔪_s is the residue field of the local ring. A geometric fiber extends this residue field to an algebraic closure.
- Base change of an affine algebraic group [definition]: For a field extension L/F and an affine F-group with coordinate Hopf algebra A, its base change to L has coordinate Hopf algebra L⊗_F A.
- Basis of a free module [definition]: A family (e_i) in a module over a commutative ring such that every element is a unique finite linear combination of the e_i with coefficients in that ring. A module with such a family is free.
- Basis of a vector space [definition]: A linearly independent family of vectors whose linear span is the whole vector space.
- Bijection between sets [definition]: A function for which each element of the codomain is the image of exactly one element of the domain.
- Bilinear map [definition]: A map of two vector-space variables that is linear in each variable separately.
- Binary operation on a set [definition]: A function S×S→S.
- Binary relation on a set [definition]: A subset R⊆S×S; writing xRy means (x,y)∈R.
- Borel σ-algebra of a topological space [definition]: The σ-algebra generated by the open subsets of the space.
- Borel σ-algebra on the real line [definition]: The σ-algebra on ℝ generated by the open intervals.
- Cardinality of a set [definition]: The size of a set, with two sets having the same cardinality when there is a bijection between them. A set has finite cardinality n when it is in bijection with {1,…,n}; infinite cardinalities are distinguished by the same bijection criterion.
- Cartesian product of sets [definition]: A×B is the set of ordered pairs (a,b) with a∈A and b∈B.
- Category [definition]: Objects and morphisms with associative composition and identity morphisms.
- Category of abelian varieties up to isogeny [construction]: Over a fixed characteristic-zero field k, abelian varieties up to isogeny have morphism spaces Hom⁰_k(A,B)=Hom_k(A,B)⊗_ℤℚ, with composition induced by composition of homomorphisms and extended ℚ-bilinearly. Every isogeny is invertible in this category.
- Cauchy sequence in a metric space [definition]: A sequence (xₙ) in a metric space (X,d) is Cauchy if for every ε>0 there is N such that d(xₙ,xₘ)<ε whenever n,m≥N.
- Characteristic of a field [definition]: The least positive integer n with n·1=0 in the field, if one exists, and zero otherwise.
- Classical cusp form with Dirichlet character [definition]: A weight-k cusp form with Dirichlet character χ modulo N is holomorphic on the upper half-plane, satisfies f((aτ+b)/(cτ+d))=χ(d)(cτ+d)^k f(τ) for matrices in Γ₀(N), and vanishes at every cusp. Equivalently it belongs to the χ-eigenspace of diamond operators on the cusp forms for Γ₁(N).
- Closed immersion of schemes [definition]: A scheme morphism i:Z→X is a closed immersion if it is a homeomorphism onto a closed subset and the structure-sheaf map O_X→i_*O_Z is surjective on stalks. Affinely it is induced by a quotient map of rings R→R/I.
- Closure of a subset of a topological space [definition]: The closure of A is the intersection of all closed subsets containing A; a set is closed when its complement is open.
- Coarse moduli scheme [construction]: For a moduli functor F of isomorphism classes of families on k-schemes, a coarse moduli scheme M has a natural transformation F→Hom_k(−,M) universal among transformations to represented scheme functors, and inducing a bijection on points over every algebraically closed extension of k. It need not carry a universal family.
- Coinvariant space of a group representation [definition]: For a linear representation ρ of a group G on a vector space V over a field F, the coinvariant space is V_G=V/span_F{ρ(g)v−v:g∈G,v∈V}. Every G-equivariant map from V to a trivial representation factors uniquely through this quotient.
- Commutative Hopf algebra over a field [definition]: A Hopf algebra over a field whose underlying algebra multiplication is commutative; its coproduct need not be cocommutative.
- Commutative algebra over a commutative ring [definition]: A commutative ring A with a specified unital ring homomorphism R→A; algebra homomorphisms commute with these structure maps.
- Commutative algebra over a field [definition]: A unital associative algebra over a field whose multiplication is commutative, equivalently a commutative ring equipped with a unital homomorphism from that field.
- Commutative ring with identity [definition]: An abelian group under addition, together with an associative commutative multiplication with identity, distributing over addition. The zero ring is allowed.
- Compact modular curve of a congruence subgroup [definition]: For a finite-index congruence subgroup Γ⊆SL₂(ℤ), its compact complex modular curve is Γ\(ℍ∪ℙ¹(ℚ)), with the Riemann-surface charts at elliptic points and cusps. Its arithmetic model and the identification of its complex points must be specified separately; the marked-point and µ_N level conventions for X₁(N) can assign different cusp fields to the same analytic labels.
- Compact topological space [definition]: A topological space in which every open cover has a finite subcover.
- Complement of a subset [definition]: For A⊆Ω, its complement Ω∖A consists of the elements of Ω that do not belong to A.
- Complete metric space [definition]: A metric space is complete if every Cauchy sequence in it converges to a point of that same space.
- Completion of a field with an absolute value [definition]: The metric completion of K for d(x,y)=|x−y|, with the field operations extended continuously. A completion is regarded as containing K through its canonical isometric embedding.
- Completion of a metric space [definition]: A complete metric space containing an isometric dense copy of the given metric space. It is unique up to an isometry fixing that copy.
- Complex conjugation [definition]: The complex conjugate of z=a+bi is z̄=a−bi.
- Complex derivative [definition]: For a complex-valued function on an open subset of C, f′(z) is the limit of (f(z+h)−f(z))/h as nonzero complex h→0, when it exists.
- Complex exponential function [definition]: The entire function exp(z)=∑ₙ₌₀∞zⁿ/n!; on imaginary arguments exp(it)=cos(t)+i sin(t).
- Complex inner-product space [definition]: A complex vector space with a positive definite Hermitian form ⟨x,y⟩, linear in x and conjugate-linear in y. Its induced norm is ∥x∥=√⟨x,x⟩.
- Complex line integral along a piecewise smooth path [definition]: For a piecewise C¹ path γ:[a,b]→ℂ and a complex function f continuous on its image, the line (contour) integral is ∫_γf(z)dz=∫_a^bf(γ(t))γ′(t)dt, summed over the finitely many C¹ pieces, with real and imaginary parts integrated separately.
- Complex multiplication of a weight-two newform [definition]: A normalized weight-two newform f has complex multiplication if there is a nontrivial quadratic Dirichlet character χ such that χ(p)a_p(f)=a_p(f) for almost all primes p. Equivalently f has a nontrivial quadratic self-twist; non-CM means no such character exists.
- Complex multiplication of an elliptic curve [definition]: An elliptic curve E in characteristic zero has complex multiplication if End_(F̄)(E) strictly contains ℤ; the extra endomorphisms form an order in an imaginary quadratic field.
- Complex number [definition]: A complex number is a pair of real numbers, written a+bi. Addition is componentwise and multiplication is (a+bi)(c+di)=(ac−bd)+(ad+bc)i, so i²=−1.
- Complex power series [definition]: A series ∑ₙ₌₀∞aₙ(z−z₀)ⁿ with complex coefficients, considered at points where its partial sums converge.
- Complex torus [definition]: A quotient V/Λ of a finite-dimensional complex vector space V by a discrete subgroup Λ generated over Z by a real basis of V, with the quotient topology and the local complex coordinates induced by translations in V.
- Composition of functions [definition]: For f:A→B and g:B→C, the composite g∘f:A→C sends x to g(f(x)).
- Congruence modulo an integer [definition]: The relation a≡b (mod m) means that m divides a−b.
- Congruence subgroup of SL₂(ℤ) [definition]: A subgroup of SL₂(ℤ) containing Γ(N)={M:M≡I moduloN} for some positive integer N.
- Congruence subgroup Γ₀(N) [definition]: For N≥1, Γ₀(N) is the subgroup of SL₂(ℤ) consisting of matrices whose lower-left entry is divisible by N. It contains Γ₁(N); the map sending a matrix to its lower-right entry modulo N identifies Γ₀(N)/Γ₁(N) with (ℤ/Nℤ)×.
- Congruence subgroup Γ₁(N) [definition]: The subgroup of SL₂(ℤ) whose matrices [[a,b],[c,d]] satisfy a≡d≡1 and c≡0 moduloN.
- Connected component of a topological space [definition]: A maximal connected subset, with connectedness understood in the subspace topology.
- Connected topological space [definition]: A topological space that is not the union of two disjoint nonempty open subsets.
- Continuous induction from a subgroup of finite index [construction]: For an open subgroup H of finite index in a profinite group G and a continuous H-action on an R-module M, Ind_H^G M can be realized as continuous functions f:G→M satisfying f(hg)=h f(g), with (g₀f)(g)=f(gg₀). It is a finite direct product of copies of M indexed by H\G; this fixes the function-model convention.
- Continuous map between topological spaces [definition]: A map is continuous if the preimage of every open subset of its target is open in its domain.
- Convergence of a sequence in a metric space [definition]: A sequence (xₙ) in a metric space (X,d) converges to x∈X if for every ε>0 there is N such that d(xₙ,x)<ε whenever n≥N.
- Convergent real series [definition]: The series ∑ₙ≥1aₙ converges to S if its sequence of partial sums converges to S.
- Coprime integers [definition]: Integers whose greatest common divisor is1.
- Cusp of a subgroup of SL₂(Z) [definition]: An orbit of a subgroup Γ⊂SL₂(Z) on Q∪{∞} under fractional linear transformations; for a congruence subgroup its cusp width is the positive translation period after sending a representative to infinity.
- Cyclic group [definition]: A group G is cyclic if there is an element g such that every element of G equals gⁿ for some integer n. A finite cyclic group has a finite order equal to the order of a generator.
- Cyclic isogeny of elliptic curves [definition]: An isogeny of elliptic curves is cyclic if its geometric kernel is a cyclic group. In characteristic zero a cyclic isogeny of degree n has geometric kernel isomorphic to ℤ/nℤ.
- Cyclotomic field [definition]: A number field ℚ(ζₙ) generated by a primitive nth root of unity.
- Decomposition group at a prime of a Galois extension [definition]: For a finite Galois extension L/F and a prime w above v, the subgroup D_w⊂Gal(L/F) of automorphisms preserving w.
- Degeneracy map between cusp-form levels [construction]: For M|N and d|N/M, the degeneracy map α_d:S_k(Γ₁(M))→S_k(Γ₁(N)) sends f(z) to f(dz), equivalently ∑a_nq^n to ∑a_nq^(dn). On a fixed character subspace it is used only when the character at level N is induced from level M.
- Dense subset of a metric space [definition]: A subset A of a metric space X is dense if every open ball centered in X with positive radius meets A.
- Derivative of a real function [definition]: The derivative f′(x) is the finite limit of (f(x+h)−f(x))/h as h→0, when it exists. Higher derivatives are defined by iteration.
- Descending filtration of a group [construction]: A descending filtration is a chain of subgroups G=F⁰G⊇F¹G⊇⋯; whenever F^(i+1)G is normal in F^iG, the ith graded quotient is Gr^iG=F^iG/F^(i+1)G. For a group scheme the filtration is by subgroup schemes and the quotient is taken as a sheaf when needed.
- Determinant of a square matrix [definition]: For an n×n matrix A over a commutative ring, det A=∑_{σ∈Sₙ}sgn(σ)∏_{i=1}ⁿA_{i,σ(i)}, with the 0×0 determinant equal to one.
- Diamond operator on Γ₁(N) modular forms [construction]: For d∈(ℤ/Nℤ)× and a weight-k modular form on Γ₁(N), choose γ∈Γ₀(N) with lower-right entry d modulo N and set ⟨d⟩f=f|ₖγ. This is independent of γ modulo Γ₁(N); the character-χ subspace satisfies ⟨d⟩f=χ(d)f, and cusp forms are preserved.
- Dimension of a vector space [definition]: The cardinality of a basis of the vector space; a finite basis with n vectors gives dimension n.
- Direct sum of abelian groups [definition]: For a family of abelian groups A_i, the subgroup of their Cartesian product consisting of tuples with finite support, with coordinatewise addition.
- Dirichlet series [definition]: A Dirichlet series is ∑_(n≥1)a_n n^(−s), with complex coefficients and n^(−s)=exp(−s log n), considered on a right half-plane where it converges.
- Discrete module for a topological group [definition]: A discrete module for a topological group G is an abelian G-module M with the discrete topology and continuous action G×M→M. Equivalently each element of M has an open stabilizer in G.
- Discrete topological space [definition]: A topological space is discrete if every subset is open.
- Discrete valuation on a field [definition]: A surjective map v:K×→Z satisfying v(xy)=v(x)+v(y) and v(x+y)≥min(v(x),v(y)), extended by v(0)=+∞.
- Discriminant of a number field [definition]: For an integral basis ω₁,…,ω_n of F, the integer d_F=det(Tr_(F/Q)(ω_iω_j))_(i,j). A change of integral basis leaves this determinant unchanged.
- Disjoint sets [definition]: Two sets are disjoint when they have no common element; a family is pairwise disjoint when every two distinct members are disjoint.
- Divisibility of integers [definition]: An integer a divides b if b=ac for some integer c.
- Division field of an elliptic curve [definition]: The n-division field F(E[n]) is the extension of F generated by the affine coordinates of all n-torsion points of an elliptic curve E/F. It is finite Galois, and its Galois group acts faithfully on E[n].
- Divisor on a smooth projective curve [definition]: A divisor on a smooth projective curve C/k is a finite integer sum D=∑n_PP of closed points. Its degree is ∑n_P[k(P):k]. After extension to an algebraic closure, geometric points are used with degree1. A divisor is effective if all n_P≥0.
- Dual isogeny of an elliptic-curve isogeny [definition]: For an isogeny φ:E→E′ of degree n, the dual isogeny φ̂:E′→E satisfies φ̂φ=[n] on E and φφ̂=[n] on E′.
- Element integral over a commutative ring [definition]: For a ring inclusion R⊂A, an element a∈A is integral over R if it satisfies a monic polynomial with coefficients in R.
- Elliptic curve over a characteristic-zero field [definition]: Over a characteristic-zero field F, an elliptic curve can be presented as a nonsingular projective cubic y²z=x³+Axz²+Bz³ with A,B∈F and 4A³+27B²≠0, with distinguished point O=[0:1:0] and the chord-and-tangent abelian group law. Curves are identified by F-isomorphisms preserving O.
- Equivalence class [definition]: For an equivalence relation ~ on X, [x]={y∈X:y~x}; the quotient set X/~ consists of these classes.
- Equivalence relation [definition]: A reflexive, symmetric and transitive binary relation.
- Euclidean space ℝᵈ [definition]: For d≥1, ℝᵈ consists of d-tuples of real numbers with distance dist(x,y)=(∑ᵢ(xᵢ−yᵢ)²)¹ᐟ².
- Euler product of a Dirichlet series [definition]: For a multiplicative coefficient function a, an absolutely convergent Dirichlet series factors as ∑a(n)n^(−s)=∏_(p prime)(∑_(j≥0)a(pʲ)p^(−js)). An Euler-product representation specifies these local factors and its domain of convergence.
- Exact sequence of modules [definition]: A sequence of homomorphisms of modules over one commutative ring is exact at a module if the image of the incoming map equals the kernel of the outgoing map; it is exact if this holds at every term.
- Extended nonnegative real numbers [definition]: The nonnegative real numbers together with a greatest element ∞. Sums involving ∞ equal ∞, and 0·∞ is taken to be 0 when integrating simple functions.
- Factorial [definition]: For n≥1, n!=1·2···n, and 0!=1.
- Family of elliptic curves [definition]: An elliptic curve over a scheme S is an abelian scheme E→S whose geometric fibers have dimension one. In particular it has a specified identity section, and its group law is part of the family; pullback gives a family over every S′→S.
- Fiber product of schemes [construction]: For X→S and Y→S, X×_S Y is the scheme with projection morphisms whose composites to S agree, universal for pairs of morphisms T→X,T→Y over S. For affine schemes Spec A,Spec B over Spec R it is Spec(A⊗_R B).
- Field [definition]: A commutative ring in which 1≠0 and every nonzero element is invertible.
- Field embedding [definition]: An injective homomorphism of fields preserving the multiplicative identity.
- Field extension [definition]: An inclusion of one field into another.
- Field of p-adic numbers [definition]: For a prime p, ℚ_p is the completion of ℚ for the p-adic absolute value |x|_p=p^{-v_p(x)}.
- Finite Galois field extension [definition]: A finite Galois extension L/k is a field extension of finite vector-space dimension which is both normal and separable. Its automorphisms fixing k form the finite Galois group Gal(L/k).
- Finite field [definition]: A field whose underlying set is finite.
- Finite morphism of schemes [definition]: A scheme morphism f:X→Y is finite if for each affine open Spec R⊆Y its inverse image is affine Spec A and A is a finitely generated R-module via f. In particular a finite group scheme over a field has finite-dimensional coordinate algebra.
- Finite set [definition]: A set X is finite if it is bijective with {1,…,n} for some natural number n≥0; its cardinality is n.
- Finite simple undirected graph [definition]: A finite simple graph is (V,E), where V is finite and E is a set of two-element subsets of V.
- Finite-dimensional vector space [definition]: A vector space having a finite basis.
- Finitely generated commutative algebra [definition]: An R-algebra A generated as an algebra by finitely many elements; equivalently a quotient of R[X₁,…,X_n] for some finite n.
